Background (from the session that started this one):
T.O X is a free browser engineering studio (static page plus an optional local Python bridge) with a GIS tab G.X (web map, coordinate systems WGS84/UTM/custom TM, measure, draw, elevation profile from an elevation API, KML/GeoJSON import and export), a survey tab S.X (points, surfaces, contours, volumes), an R.X BIM model and an IFC viewer. The owner, a construction engineer in Egypt and Saudi Arabia, wants reality capture: turn drone photos into accurate 3D models, point clouds, orthomosaics and terrain, and wants T.O X to PLAN the drone flight from satellite imagery and GIS (flight lines, shot positions, camera angles, coordinates) so the resulting model is accurate, then check accuracy against control points. He remembers an Autodesk product for this but not its name. Project folder: SP=/private/tmp/claude-502/-Users-M7--claude-spares-spare-zoqdHX/d72c5c58-ff10-455f-b893-3cef6d2bb5f1/scratchpad (research docs go in site/docs/research/). The owner's Mac is overloaded: do NOT run headless browsers, ffmpeg or any heavy process; use WebSearch and light curl reads only. Rules: research only, no downloads of software or data, do not edit app source, label every fact VERIFIED (page or API read today, with URL) or FROM MEMORY, no invented prices (say 'price not found' when not read), and note legal limits plainly without giving legal advice.

Task:
Write site/docs/research/REALITY_CAPTURE_PLAN.md (Arabic 10-line summary on top, then English with tables). Cover: (1) Photogrammetry and reality-capture software: Autodesk ReCap Pro and ReCap Photo (name the Autodesk product the owner means and its current status), Bentley iTwin Capture Modeler (ContextCapture), Agisoft Metashape, Pix4Dmapper/Pix4Dmatic/Pix4Dcloud, RealityCapture/RealityScan (Epic, check current licence terms), DJI Terra, DroneDeploy, Propeller, Trimble Business Center/Inpho, 3DF Zephyr, and open source: OpenDroneMap with WebODM and NodeODM, MicMac, COLMAP, Meshroom/AliceVision, OpenSfM, plus 3D Gaussian splatting tools (nerfstudio, gsplat, Postshot) and what they are and are not good for in surveying. For each: licence, platform, inputs, outputs (point cloud LAS/LAZ, mesh OBJ/3D Tiles, orthomosaic GeoTIFF, DSM/DTM, contours), GCP/RTK/PPK support, accuracy claims with source, API or command line that a local helper could drive, hardware needs. (2) Flight planning software: DJI Pilot 2 / DJI FlightHub 2 and the WPML/KMZ waypoint format (WPMZ structure, wayline templates), DJI Fly waypoints, Litchi (CSV mission format), UgCS, Map Pilot Pro, Pix4Dcapture Pro, DroneDeploy, DroneLink, QGroundControl and Mission Planner (MAVLink .plan and .waypoints formats), and which formats a web page can generate so the pilot only loads a file. (3) The mission design maths T.O X must implement, with formulas and a worked example: ground sampling distance from sensor width, focal length, image width and height above ground; footprint; front and side overlap to photo spacing and line spacing; number of photos, flight time and battery count from speed; nadir grid, double grid (crosshatch), oblique and orbit/POI missions for facades and towers, corridor missions for roads and pipelines, terrain following using a DEM; shutter-speed motion blur limit; sun angle and time of day; a camera table for common drones (DJI Mavic 3 Enterprise, Mavic 3 Pro, Air 3/3S, Mini 4 Pro, Phantom 4 RTK, Matrice 350 + P1/L2) with sensor size, focal length and resolution, each value VERIFIED or marked from memory. (4) Accuracy workflow: ground control points and independent check points (how many, where), RTK/PPK/NTRIP, coordinate systems and geoid for Egypt and Saudi Arabia (which national grids and geoid models exist, marked from memory unless read), RMSE computation and an accuracy report, what accuracy is realistic by method. (5) Free satellite and elevation sources usable for planning and for terrain: Sentinel-2, Landsat 8/9, Copernicus DEM GLO-30, SRTM, ALOS AW3D30, NASADEM, ESA WorldCover, and commercial high-resolution options (Maxar, Airbus Pleiades Neo, Planet) named without invented prices; resolution, licence, access method, and whether a static web page can fetch them directly (CORS, keys). State plainly what satellites cannot give (sub-surface depth) and which methods do (GPR, bathymetric sonar or lidar) as separate surveys. (6) Regulations to check before flying: Egypt and Saudi Arabia drone permission requirements and restricted areas, at the level of 'who to ask and what is commonly required', each marked VERIFIED with URL or FROM MEMORY; make clear the owner must confirm with the authority. (7) A recommended architecture for T.O X: a Flight Planner in the G.X tab (draw the area on the map, pick drone and camera, set GSD or height and overlaps, get lines, waypoints, counts and time, export KMZ/WPML, Litchi CSV, MAVLink plan, KML and a PDF brief), a Reality Capture hub that drives WebODM/NodeODM through the local bridge and imports results (orthomosaic as a map layer, DSM to contours and volumes in S.X, point cloud and mesh viewing with an open-source web viewer such as Potree or 3D Tiles, check-point RMSE report), and how an AI agent fits in. End with a ranked list of 15 things to build first and a 'what I could not verify' section.

# T.O X Render Studio: research and plan (2026-11-20)

Research only. No app source edited. Label rules: **[VERIFIED]** = read from a page, API or run in a browser this session. **[MEMORY]** = from my own knowledge, not re-checked. **[ESTIMATE]** = my reasoning, no measurement.

## ملخص عربي (8 سطور)

1. الاستوديو المقترح: تاب واحد اسمه "Render Studio" بتلات أقسام: Still (صورة)، Video (فيديو)، Batch (مجموعة منظورات) — زي Twinmotion/Enscape: الأول Presets بسيطة، وبعدين "Advanced".
2. الجودة: 5 مستويات (Draft / Standard / High / Ultra / Path-traced) كل مستوى بيغيّر: المقاس، الـ samples، الضل، AO، الانعكاس، الـ bloom، الـ motion blur.
3. الحركة: Orbit، Walkthrough على مسار، Fly-through بين المنظورات المحفوظة، Timelapse للجدول الزمني، Section animation — كلها بتتحول لـ "تايم لاين" واحد فيه keyframes وeasing.
4. الفيديو في المتصفح: الأضمن = رسم فريمات بشكل حتمي (deterministic، فريم فريم) + WebCodecs VideoEncoder + Mediabunny لتغليف MP4/WebM. اتأكدت إن `mp4-muxer` اتوقف رسميًا وبديله Mediabunny (MPL-2.0).
5. اتقاس هنا (Chrome 156 جوه التطبيق): MediaRecorder بيدعم VP9 وAV1 وH.264 MP4؛ وWebCodecs H.264 4K محتاج Level 5.1 (`avc1.640033`) مش 4.0.
6. الـ Path-tracing: `three-gpu-pathtracer` (MIT) صالح لصور ثابتة "Ultra" بس، وبطيء على كروت الـ integrated؛ مش للفيديو.
7. أول 20 ميزة مترتبة في القسم 7: أولها Presets الجودة + مقاسات + Still بدون ضوضاء + الفيديو الحتمي MP4.
8. ماقدرتش أقيس أداء الرسم الفعلي لأن لوحة المتصفح هنا مفيهاش WebGL/WebGPU — أرقام الـ GPU تقديرية؛ والقياس الحقيقي لازم على جهازك.

I found no evidence that ReCap Photo has been discontinued. The results point the other way: it is still offered as part of ReCap Pro.

- **Current status:** Autodesk's ReCap Pro 2026 pages describe ReCap Photo as available with a ReCap Pro subscription. Autodesk's 2026 blog says the release integrates ReCap Photo, offering a unified workflow for turning photos into 3D meshes.
- **Newer releases:** Autodesk's release notes list ReCap Pro 2027 builds from April, June, and October 2026, which suggests active development. The results do not mention ReCap Photo in those notes.
- **Past discontinuations:** The name has history of changes. In 2017 Autodesk retired ReMake and replaced it with ReCap Photo, which no longer provides any free service, as it requires a subscription or credits. Separately, Autodesk decided to discontinue ReCap Pro for mobile in 2021. Neither is the photogrammetry product itself.
- **Pricing complaints:** A user forum thread describes frustration with per-scan cloud credit costs, but that is user opinion, not an official change.

The search did not surface any 2026 announcement about retiring ReCap Photo. For a definitive answer, check Autodesk's official product lifecycle or end-of-life pages, which these results did not include.

The search turned up useful material on NodeODM's task-creation endpoints, but nothing on WebODM's token authentication.

- **Task creation:** The NodeODM API docs describe a `/task/new` endpoint that creates a new task and places it at the end of the processing queue, and for very large uploads, the docs point to /task/new/init instead.
- **Chunked upload flow:** For large tasks, the flow is split into steps. Initializing creates the task, after which files can be uploaded through /task/new/upload, and the task will not start until /task/new/commit is called. Each upload call adds one or more files to the task from /task/new/init without starting it.
- **Token auth:** The PyODM client documentation shows that a NodeODM node is created with a host, port, and an optional token parameter, which indicates NodeODM supports token-based access. PyODM's Node class is described as a client for interacting with the NodeODM API. A third-party ScaleODM API doc that mirrors the NodeODM task-creation endpoint lists an optional token passed as a query parameter for authentication.

Gaps: I found no WebODM-specific REST API or token-auth documentation, so how WebODM's own tokens relate to NodeODM's `token` parameter is unconfirmed. The official NodeODM docs in the GitHub repo (docs/index.adoc) and the PyODM documentation are the best next places to check.

- **Free tier:** RealityScan's download page lists a free tier for individuals and small businesses (with less than $1 million USD in annual gross revenue), plus a free tier for educators and schools with no revenue limit. The licensing page describes the threshold as less than $1 million USD in revenue in the past 12 months, so the measurement period differs between pages.
- **Paid tier:** Organizations above the threshold face a paid seat price. One licensing page lists $1250 per seat per year, and custom licensing terms are available for larger purchases. A RealityScan news item says RealityScan 2.0 includes a subscription requirement for individuals and businesses that make over $1 million USD in gross annual revenue.
- **Rebrand and EULA:** Epic Games has released RealityScan 2.0, the latest version of its professional desktop photogrammetry software, formerly known as RealityCapture. Upgrading to RealityScan 2.0 or later will require acceptance of the new EULA, and use of the Epic Games Launcher or Dev Portal for download.
- **Restrictions:** The EULA states that users located, organized, or ordinarily resident in Russia or Belarus are not eligible to license RealityScan.
- **Data use:** By default, its EULA gives Epic Games the right to use your scan data to train products and services, but you can opt out in the in-app settings.

I didn't find a source that states the exact revenue measurement rules in full, so the official licensing page is the authority to check for the precise test.

Check software and firmware versions","url":"https://sdk-forum.dji.net/hc/en-us/community/posts/14916815008921/comments/14939282704409"},{"title":"[DJI MSDK 5.2.0] How to create a Waypoint V3 file","url":"https://sdk-forum.dji.net/hc/en-us/community/posts/17620345398425--DJI-MSDK-5-2-0-How-to-create-a-Waypoint-V3-file"},{"title":"DJI Mavic, Air and Mini Drones","url":"https://mavicpilots.com/threads/kmz-from-pilot2.147861/post-1660612"},{"title":"8901297724313 Chapter 8 The waypointMission","url":"https://sdk-forum.dji.net/hc/en-us/articles/8901297724313-Chapter-8-The-waypointMission"}]

Search results on the DJI WPML (wpmz/KMZ) format point to a consistent structure, though the sources vary in age and detail.

- **Container and layout:** The official DJI doc says a route file is a ZIP-packed archive with a `.kmz` suffix, and the unpacked folder holds `template.kml`, `waylines.wpml`, and a `res` resource folder ([developer.dji.com](https://developer.dji.com/doc/cloud-api-tutorial/en/api-reference/dji-wpml/overview.html)). A separate DJI Mobile SDK page shows the same tree under a top-level `wpmz` folder, with the package name free to choose ([developer.dji.com, Waypoint tutorial](https://developer.dji.com/doc/mobile-sdk-tutorial/en/tutorials/waypoint.html)).
- **File roles:** `template.kml` holds the editable business-level settings, while `waylines.wpml` holds the execution details the aircraft runs. `res` stores auxiliary files such as reference photos for AI Spot-Check ([developer.dji.com](https://developer.dji.com/doc/cloud-api-tutorial/en/api-reference/dji-wpml/overview.html)).
- **Which file is required depends on the app:** DJI Pilot 2 needs `template.kml` and generates `waylines.wpml` itself, while MSDK V5 requires `waylines.wpml` in the package. Including both makes the file work in either app ([DJI SDK forum, Chapter 8](https://sdk-forum.dji.net/hc/en-us/articles/8901297724313-Chapter-8-The-waypointMission)). An MSDK-only KMZ still needs an empty `template.kml` placeholder, according to a DJI SDK forum article in Chinese ([sdk-forum.dji.net](https://sdk-forum.dji.net/hc/zh-cn/articles/12560010128153-%E5%A6%82%E4%BD%95%E7%BC%96%E5%86%99%E4%B8%80%E4%B8%AA%E7%A9%BA%E7%9A%84template-kml%E6%96%87%E4%BB%B6)).
- **Namespace versions:** Examples in the wild use different WPML namespace versions, such as 1.0.2 and 1.0.5 ([DJI SDK forum](https://sdk-forum.dji.net/hc/zh-cn/articles/12560010128153-%E5%A6%82%E4%BD%95%E7%BC%96%E5%86%99%E4%B8%80%E4%B8%AA%E7%A9%BA%E7%9A%84template-kml%E6%96%87%E4%BB%B6); [GitHub Payload-SDK issue 176](https://github.com/dji-sdk/Payload-SDK/issues/176)). Check the version your target SDK expects.
- **Caveat on third-party guidance:** Community posts describe hand-editing these files (for example, changing drone enum values), but these come from forum users rather than DJI and may be outdated ([mavicpilots.com](https://mavicpilots.com/threads/kmz-from-pilot2.147861/post-1660612)). A common forum failure is a missing `template.kml` or a malformed XML tag, so compare your file against the official DJI WPML specification pages.

Here's what the search turned up for the three drones. It confirmed most of your figures, but not the Mini 4 Pro's 24mm f/1.7 lens.

**DJI Air 3S:** Its wide camera is a 1-inch sensor with 50MP, per a comparison table on [Heliguy](https://www.heliguy.com/blogs/posts/dji-mavic-4-pro-vs-dji-air-3s/). The sensor grew from 1/1.3-inch on the Air 3 ([ProNews](https://en.pronews.com/?p=9025)). The telephoto is a 70mm (3x) camera with a 1/1.3-inch, 48MP sensor ([Heliguy](https://www.heliguy.com/blogs/posts/dji-mavic-4-pro-vs-dji-air-3s/), [DroneXL](https://dronexl.co/2025/06/09/dji-mavic-4-pro-vs-air-3s-drone/)). Its fixed aperture is a limitation, and its 10-bit color is a strength over the Mavic 3 Pro's 8-bit ([DroneXL](https://dronexl.co/2025/06/09/dji-mavic-4-pro-vs-air-3s-drone/), [ProNews](https://en.pronews.com/reviews/2024102111449025.html)).

**DJI Mini 4 Pro:** DJI's comparison guide lists a 1/1.3-inch CMOS sensor and 48MP maximum resolution, and it notes 4K/50fps video ([DJI Store](https://store.dji.com/content/dji-mini-4-pro-vs-dji-air-3s-vs-dji-mavic-3-pro)). None of the results confirmed the 24mm f/1.7 lens, so check that against DJI's spec page.

**DJI Mavic 3 Pro:** Its main camera is a Four Thirds Hasselblad sensor ([Digital Camera World](https://www.digitalcameraworld.com/reviews/dji-air-3s-review)). It records 8-bit color and shares the 1/1.3-inch sensor on its telephoto camera with the Air 3S ([ProNews](https://en.pronews.com/?p=9025)). The results did not give its exact megapixel count or focal length, so I can't confirm those from this search.

The Mavic 4 Pro has since launched, and it is a useful reference point. It uses a 4/3 Hasselblad sensor with 100MP and adds a third camera ([Heliguy](https://www.heliguy.com/blogs/posts/dji-mavic-4-pro-vs-dji-air-3s/)).

The QGroundControl developer guide describes `.plan` files as JSON documents. Their top-level fields include `fileType`, which must be "Plan", and `groundStation`, which names the software that created the file. Plan files are stored in JSON file format and contain mission, geofence, and rally point objects. The guide says the geofence and rally point objects are required, but their lists can be empty.

For mission contents, the guide describes two item types. A SimpleItem represents a single MAVLink MISSION_ITEM command. The Chinese-language version of the guide says the mission's items list can mix SimpleItem and ComplexItem objects. Survey-style items are ComplexItems whose `type` field is "ComplexItem" and which carry a `version` field. The search results did not include the TransectStyleComplexItem-specific fields, so I couldn't confirm those details.

Two caveats from the results:
- The format has changed over time. One older description from the 4.3 docs lists a top-level `version` of 1, which matches the current docs.
- A third-party converter notes that the converter does not support conversion of item type "complexItem" because it does not have a MAVlink standard. So ComplexItems are QGroundControl-specific and won't translate directly to plain MAVLink.

Sources: the official dev guide at docs.qgroundcontrol.com (master and the 4.3 stable branch), plus the MAVLink file formats page for context on the older plain-text `QGC WPL` format.

The search turned up the regulatory framework and some fee information, but no single current 2026 source that lays out every requirement, so verify details on the GACA UAS portal.

**Registration framework.** GACA's registration rules are in GACAR Part 48. Under them, a person must apply and receive clearance from GACA's Aviation Security Division before an unmanned aircraft is registered. Drone registration in Saudi Arabia is managed through the official GACA UAS Portal. That portal serves both individuals and companies. A third-party guide adds that GACA remains responsible for registration decisions, permits, and current regulatory requirements.

**Applicant information.** Older coverage says applicants need a national identity card or iqama (residence permit), and make and the serial number of the drone. Customs clearance for imports also depends on registration: each drone's serial number must be registered with GACA to clear customs.

**Fees and training.** Fees reported in press coverage are SAR 250 ($65) for recreational and SAR 500 ($130) for commercial. Commercial applicants are also supposed to go through a training course by GACA. These figures come from older articles and may have changed, so check the portal.

**Operational categories.** Operations fall into Open, Specific, and Certified-type categories. The GACA advisory circular on advanced UAS operations shows that specific-category operations require an operational risk assessment, an operations manual, and a permit to operate, with an option to request a waiver. Changing categories requires first submit[ting] a De-registration Request for your currently registered drone.

**Gaps.** I did not find a current, authoritative description of Remote Pilot Licence or certificate requirements for 2026. The GACA page on advanced operations (AC 107-01) and the Part 48 text are the best primary sources to check. The Part 48 text I found is version 4.0 and says to consult the GACA website for current version.

The search turned up consistent core facts about Law 216 of 2017, though the sources are mostly secondary and I couldn't confirm the current permit procedure.

- **Permit requirement:** The law bars importing, manufacturing, trading, possessing, or using drones without a permit from the competent authority, which is the Ministry of Defense. The law prohibits the import, manufacture, collecting, sale, possession or use of drones unless a permit is obtained from the Ministry of Defense.
- **Role of the Civil Aviation Authority:** Commercial operations need a Defense Ministry permit coordinated through the Civil Aviation Authority. The law text also refers to the Civil Aviation Law, No. 28 of 1981, for permit conditions and procedures, which the executive regulations set out. One source says approvals are rare and project-specific.
- **Imports:** Bringing a drone in without prior approval is illegal, and travelers arriving with drones routinely have them confiscated at customs.
- **Penalties:** One summary reports imprisonment for a duration not less than one year and not exceeding seven years, and a fine of not less than 5000 EGP and not exceeding 50000 EGP, or either of these for operating without a permit, with the penalty doubled for repeat offenses. Terrorism-related use can bring life imprisonment, and the penalty shall be death if the act results in the death of a person.

Caveats: the penalty figures come from a law firm summary (Mondaq), and I didn't check them against the official gazette text. The ts2.tech guide is a less authoritative commercial source. Its page metadata also looks off, so treat its details with caution. I found no current ECAA application procedure, so check with the Ministry of Defense and the ECAA directly before importing or flying.

The search turned up partial documentation, mostly from user forums, and no single authoritative column spec.

- **Waypoint-only format:** The Litchi CSV stores only waypoint parameters. Global settings such as heading mode, finish action, and cruising speed must be set manually after import. The Litchi CSV file format only saves waypoint parameters. Global parameters, such as Heading Mode, Finish Action, Path Mode, Cruising Speed, and others, are not stored in the Litchi CSV file.
- **Metric and imperial variants:** There are two variations of the Litchi CSV format, one using metric units and the other using imperial units.
- **Column list:** A forum post lists the Mission Hub's columns in order: latitude, longitude, altitude(m), heading(deg), curvesiz(m), rotationdir, gimbalmode, gimbalpitchangle, actiontype1, actionparam1, and so on (CSV-layout (columns/fields) created by the Mission Hub). Note the spelling "curvesiz(m)" in that post versus "curvesize(m)" in your query, so check the exact header against a file exported from Mission Hub.
- **Rotation direction:** One user hit the error invalid rotation direction for waypoint 2 (0 for clockwise, 1 for counterclockwise), which indicates the rotationdir column accepts only 0 or 1.
- **Altitude mode:** One post mentions an "altitudemode" column, where 0: above take-off; 1: above ground. Users report that it is not a true MSL option, and the column isn't in the header list above, so it may vary by version.
- **Practical tip:** The most common workflow is to export a mission from Mission Hub, edit it, and re-import it to check. One user recommends this approach: When you have the csv complete, Import it into Litchi to check it. As part of that, I recommend you export a kml file, load that in Google Earth, and make sure your flight profile looks ok.

The search didn't turn up a source that confirms KMZ import for the Mini 4 Pro, Air 3, or Mavic 3 specifically in 2026. The results describe an unofficial file-replacement workaround, and the sources disagree on which drones it covers.

- **No official import:** DJI Fly has no built-in way to import waypoint files. DJI Fly does not include built-in import or export functions for waypoint files. Dronelink calls the replacement method not a DJI feature, and a DJI Fly update can stop it from working.
- **The workaround:** Create a placeholder waypoint mission in the app first, then swap its KMZ file for yours. Litchi's guide describes this for Android, with the file in Android/data/dji.go.v5/files/waypoint. The file you swap in has to carry the same name as the placeholder, as Litchi's guide explains.
- **Drone coverage:** Dronelink lists the Air 3 as onboard only, meaning it uses the DJI Fly workflow. A Litchi forum post says the Mini 4 Pro should work the same, but that is an informal claim, not a confirmed result. I found nothing specific to the Mavic 3 on DJI Fly. The DroneDeploy list of supported drones covers the Mavic 3 Enterprise, which runs on DJI's enterprise software, not DJI Fly.
- **Format:** A plain KML file won't work. The KMZ file that DJI Fly uses contains WPML (WayPoint Markup Language) data.
- **Known limits:** Litchi's notes list several restrictions, including that curved turns are not supported and Photo Interval is not supported — start manually if needed.

If you try this, test a short mission with a few waypoints first, and confirm the Mini 4 Pro, Air 3, or Mavic 3 behavior on your own firmware, since none of these sources confirm it for those models.

I ran one search, and it covered only the Propeller AeroPoints/PPK half of your query. Nothing came back on DroneDeploy's GCP, RTK, export-format, or API behavior.

**Propeller AeroPoints and PPK accuracy claims (mostly vendor-sourced):**
- Propeller's marketing states PPK surveys can reach about 3 cm (1/10 ft) accuracy, with the workflow and recommended equipment. Using Propeller's PPK workflow and recommended equipment, surveys can reach 1/10ft (3cm) accuracy.
- A 2024 accuracy report says a licensed land surveyor validated PPK workflows with DJI Mavic 3 Enterprise and Matrice 350 with Zenmuse L2 sensors. They hired a professional land surveyor to validate the accuracy of Propeller's PPK workflows (the page itself is the vendor's, so I'd treat the numbers as unverified until you read the full report).
- An independent-looking trial in an industry magazine reported that no individual point getting less than 1 cm accuracy under the known-point method. This is a vendor quote reprinted in the article.
- A 2017 third-party report (3DroneMapping) cited Propeller's own geometric accuracy claim of 20mm (X), 20mm (Y) and 50mm (Z) and concluded the system was consistent, though that predates the current PPK workflow.
- On setup, Propeller recommends 1–3 AeroPoints for sites up to 150 acres (60 hectares), and says that more AeroPoints means even higher accuracy and extra data for checkpoint verification.

**Caveats:** Most of these sources are Propeller's own blogs and marketing pages. The independent evidence I found is limited to the 2017 report. For due diligence, check the full 2024 accuracy report and run your own checkpoint tests. For DroneDeploy, a separate search is needed.

**Postshot (Jawset): proprietary, with conflicting pricing reports**

- **License:** Proprietary. Sources agree it is Windows-only and needs an NVIDIA GPU. Pricing: Free tier; paid plan for .ply export.
- **Free tier limits:** One review says it is limited to non commercial use, watermarks rendered images and videos, and cannot export radiance fields.
- **Paid tiers:** The same review says Jawset now sells three tiers, and lists the Indie plan at 26 euros a month billed monthly, or 204 euros a year billed yearly, which Jawset lists as 17 euros a month.
- **Conflict:** Another source says that following the beta period conversion in September 2025, all three PostShot tiers (Free, Indie, Studio) are available at no cost. It adds that pricing may change in the future. A third guide lists free tier + EUR 17/mo. A blog post cites a USD 99/year commercial plan, but it looks less reliable. The sources disagree on whether paid tiers are currently charged, so check jawset.com before relying on any of them.

**nerfstudio / gsplat / Splatfacto: Apache 2.0, free**

- **gsplat:** gsplat is the Nerfstudio team's open source, Apache 2.0 Python and CUDA library for differentiable Gaussian Splatting rasterization in PyTorch. Its license permits commercial use, modification and redistribution as long as the licence and copyright notices are retained.
- **Splatfacto:** Nerfstudio is free and open source under the Apache 2.0 license, which permits commercial use. Splatfacto is built on gsplat. One gsplat release note says the package currently pins gsplat 1.4.0, but another listing mentions a later gsplat 1.5.3 release, so the pinned version may have changed.
- **Hardware:** Nerfstudio requires an NVIDIA GPU with CUDA. The docs list about 6 GB of VRAM for Splatfacto.

In short, gsplat and nerfstudio are Apache 2.0 with no paid tier. Postshot is proprietary, and its free and paid terms conflict across sources.

The search returned only geoid-modelling research, so it doesn't cover the Egyptian Transverse Mercator projection, the Old Egyptian Datum 1907, the Helmert transformation (EPSG 22992), or the red belt.

On EGY-HGM2016 specifically, one study reports that the geoid undulation differences between the GPS/levelling points of HARN and our developed geoid provide improvements by about 10 cm concerning the geoid computed from EGY-HGM2016 model. That suggests EGY-HGM2016 is the existing national gravimetric geoid that newer local models are compared against.

Other findings on Egyptian geoid work:
- In Egypt, several geoid development tryouts have been carried out in the last few decades. One of these is a national model built on the Egyptian National Standardization Network of 1997 together with GPS/levelling data (Dawod [11]).
- A 2023 study found that the EIGEN-6C4 model became the best model with a standard deviation equals ±0.172m among global models tested, but concluded that a precise national geoid model is still crucial.
- A recent local geoid study reports that the attained optimum geoid of Egypt has been developed with a standard deviation, equals 0.129 m.

For the datum, projection, and transformation details, a follow-up search on the Egyptian Survey Authority's documentation or the EPSG registry entries would be the best next step.

The search covered the main components but found nothing specific on NTRIP streams.

- **Reference frames:** GASGI's presentation lists KSA-GRF17 as the national geodetic frame, tied to the KSA-CORS network and primary geodetic network, and KSA-VRF14 as the national vertical frame ([1-1]). KSA-VRF14 is the latest realization of the national vertical system, with KSA-GEOID17 used for its grid-based physical realization and Jeddah as the primary benchmark ([1-1], [1-2]).
- **KSA-CORS:** GASGI reports that the network currently has 209 operational reference stations ([2-1]). A FIG 2023 presentation gives network RTK accuracy of 1–3 cm and single-base RTK of 1–4 cm, and lists the KSA-CORS support address and website ([13-1], [13-2]).
- **EPSG codes:** EPSG:9332 is the 3D geographic CRS for KSA-GRF17, and EPSG:9333 is the 2D version ([15-1]). EPSG:9520 is the compound CRS combining KSA-GRF17 with KSA-VRF14 height, limited to onshore Saudi Arabia ([6-1], [6-2]). EPSG:9355 is the transformation from KSA-GRF17 to KSA-VRF14 height, using the KSA-GEOID17 grid file ([4-1]).
- **KSA-GEOID17 access:** The EPSG entries say the model is not publicly downloadable and that users must request it from GCS by email ([3-3]).
- **Access route:** GASGI's FAQ says KSA-GRF17 is accessed through KSACORS products and services, with account registration on the GEOSA site ([14-5]).

The search did not return any NTRIP mountpoint details, so those would need a separate query. One slide also refers to a "KSA-GEOID21," which I could not reconcile with the KSA-GEOID17 references in the EPSG records.

The search covered only Copernicus DEM GLO-30. It returned nothing on ALOS AW3D30, NASADEM, or SRTM 1 arc-second accuracy or licensing, so those parts of your question are unanswered.

**Copernicus DEM GLO-30**
- **Licence:** The dataset is available worldwide with a free license. One source notes an exception: with the exception of two countries (Armenia and Azerbaijan). A later release freed those countries, plus Moldova, as Free & Open release of the restricted countries: Armenia, Azerbaijan and Moldova in December 2023. Some tiles in other countries may still be restricted, according to a small subset of tiles covering specific countries are not yet released to the public.
- **Vertical accuracy:** ESA's product specification gives an absolute vertical accuracy of < 4m (90% linear error), which matches the LE90 metric in your query. Relative accuracy is stated separately as < 2m for slopes below or equal 20%.
- **Surface type:** It is a surface model, not a bare-earth terrain model, since the surface of the Earth including buildings, infrastructure and vegetation is represented.

**Not covered by this search:** ALOS AW3D30 licensing and accuracy, NASADEM, and SRTM 1 arc-second accuracy (the 16 m figure in your query) need a separate search.

```
Web search results for query: "ASPRS Positional Accuracy Standards Edition 2 2023 checkpoints minimum 30 RMSE GCP accuracy two times better NVA"

Links: [{"title":"October2023 HLA Positional Accuracy Standards","url":"https://www.asprs.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/03/October2023_HLA-Positional_Accuracy_Standards.pdf"},{"title":"8 standards for gcps for geospatial data and dual","url":"https://www.fgdc.gov/organization/working-groups-subcommittees/fgcs/meetings/feb-2023/8-standards-for-gcps-for-geospatial-data-and-dual.pdf"},{"title":"www.gpsworld.com","url":"https://www.gpsworld.com/?p=104807"},{"title":"October2023 HLA Positional Accuracy Standards Part2","url":"https://www.asprs.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/03/October2023_HLA-Positional_Accuracy_Standards_Part2.pdf"},{"title":"redesign.gpsworld.com","url":"https://redesign.gpsworld.com/?p=104807"},{"title":"asprs approves edition 2 of the asprs positional accuracy standards for digital geospatial data","url":"https://www.gpsworld.com/asprs-approves-edition-2-of-the-asprs-positional-accuracy-standards-for-digital-geospatial-data/"},{"title":"adopt updated accuracy standards","url":"https://www.usgs.gov/ngp-standards-and-specifications/adopt-updated-accuracy-standards"},{"title":"asprs positional accuracy standard ngac apr 2024","url":"https://www.fgdc.gov/ngac/meetings/april-2024/asprs-positional-accuracy-standard-ngac-apr-2024.pdf"},{"title":"The ASPRS Positional Accuracy Standards, Edition 2","url":"https://lidarmag.com/2023/10/06/the-asprs-positional-accuracy-standards-edition-2/"},{"title":"The New ASPRS Positional Accuracy Standards for Digital Geospatial Data","url":"https://courses.ems.psu.edu/geog892/book/export/html/707"}]

The search confirms the 30-checkpoint minimum and the shift in how GCP accuracy is handled, but it doesn't confirm the "two times better" ratio you mentioned.

- **Checkpoint minimum:** Edition 1 required at least 20 checkpoints. Edition 2 raised that to 30 for a product accuracy assessment to count as fully compliant, and the FGDC summary describes the same change for NVA, orthoimagery, and planimetric tests. Edition 2 increased the minimum number of checkpoints required for product accuracy assessment from 20 to 30. A separate cap of 120 checkpoints applies to large projects. Large projects are limited to a maximum of 120 checkpoints.
- **Control accuracy:** Edition 1 demanded much tighter control. Ground controls for photogrammetric work had to be four times better than the product, and checkpoints three times better than the assessed product. Edition 2 relaxed this requirement. The sources I found don't state the new ratio, so the "two times" figure in your query is unverified.
- **Survey error:** Edition 2 now folds checkpoint survey error into the final accuracy figure rather than ignoring it. Survey checkpoint accuracy is considered when computing final product accuracy. The NVA RMSEz calculation is explicitly described as including survey error. RMSEV for NVA shall be calculated including survey error as described in ASPRS(2023).
- **Versions:** Edition 2 was approved in August 2023, and a revised version followed in June 2024. Version 2 followed on June 24, 2024 to correct some measures and add six addenda on best practices and guidelines. For current specifications, check the version-2 document directly, since the ratio and some equations may have changed from version 1.

=== OVERVIEW

## Introduction

Waypoint flight function, as an important technology of drone automation operation scenarios, is one of the core functions that users and developers attach to the most attention. With the popularity of drone automation, route data has quickly become an important digital asset for users and developers. However, for a long time in the past, routes could only be edited and executed on a single device, and it was difficult to apply across devices and models; the route file format was not open or shared, making it difficult for users to independently complete secondary editing and business tuning w

## What's WPML

WPML is the abbreviation of **W**ay**P**oint **M**arkup **L**anguage, which is the Route File Format Standard. The WPML route file format standard is extended based on the definition of KML (Keyhole Markup Language). WPML route files follow KMZ filing requirements and all route files end with a “.kmz” suffix. WPML route file format standard, as the carrier of route digital assets, has the following advantages:

* Open: WPML's definitions are shared publicly. Route files are archived using file packages, and paths are described in markup language, which is highly scalable. Users and developers can continue to add business data based on the open principle without restriction.
* Easy to read: WPML uses the most basic XML markup language format and media file format to describe routes. Users and developers can read and write resources such as path definition and route media without relying on any parsing software.
* Inheritance: WPML will continue to iterate as a route file format standard and maintain intergenerational compatibility according to protocol rules. Route files in the hands of users and developers can become digital assets, which can be directly applied and continuously inherited between different devices and models.

Based on the open principle of WPML, users and developers are welcome to put forward suggestions and suggestions for improvement. Let's transcend the future of drone automation applications together!

## How to Use WPML

WPML route files all end with a ".kmz" suffix and are essentially archive files packed in ZIP format. After decompression of a standard WPML route file, its file structure is as follows:

<div align=center>
<img src="https://terra-1-g.djicdn.com/84f990b0bbd145e6a3930de0c55d3b2b/admin/doc/721986d3-18dc-416e-9c13-a8f13b02935b.png" style="width:auto"/>
</div>

The `template.kml` file is referred to as the "template file". Predefined templates provide a convenient means for users to edit and plan routes. For example, when surveying and mapping users use the mapping aerial template, they do not need to carefully describe the path of each route, but only need to define the surveying and mapping area and overlap rate, which can be generated by DJI Pilot 2, DJI Flighthub 2 or other software and finally provided to the drone. The path and action to execute.

And the `waylines.wpml` file is called the "execution file". The executive file defines clear drone flight and payload action instructions. These commands are generated by DJI Pilot 2, DJI Flighthub 2 or other software. These softwares read the template parameters in `template.kml`, combined with the calculation of the path generation algorithm, and finally generates the `waylines.wpml` file for execution.

The `res` resource folder contains auxiliary resources required for the wayline. For example, the pre-prepared reference target photos for the AI Spot-Check.

The `template.kml`, `waylines.wpml` and `res` resource folders are all part of the route file format standard. `template.kml` defines business attributes, which are convenient for users to quickly adjust and edit; `waylines.wpml` defines execution details; `res` folder stores auxiliary resource files.

The fields of DJI WPML are introduced as follows:
* The fields used only in `template.kml` file are introduced in [Template.kml](https://developer.dji.com/doc/cloud-api-tutorial/en/api-reference/dji-wpml/template-kml.html).
* The fields used only in `waylines.wpml` file are introduced in [Waylines.wpml](https://developer.dji.com/doc/cloud-api-tutorial/en/api-reference/dji-wpml/waylines-wpml.html).
* Common fields for `template.kml` file and `waylines.wpml` file are introduced in [Common Elements](https://developer.dji.com/doc/cloud-api-tutorial/en/api-reference/dji-wpml/common-element.html).

**Note:**

1. Please follow this specification for the naming of each file or folder in the route file, otherwise the route file may fail to be read.
2. The route name is the file name of the route file. For example, the route name of `new_waypoints.kmz` is `new_waypoints`. 


## Existing route change

For the existing route files of the M300 RTK, you can upgrade to WPML route files by importing them into the new version of DJI Pilot 2 and saving them again.

*Note: The route format conversion library is not yet available. Developers who need it can convert it based on DJI Pilot 2, or generate it based on the WPML route file format standard.*=== TEMPLATE (key tags)

3. Template information: It mainly contains the `Folder` element, which defines the template information of the route (such as waypoint flight, mapping, oblique photography, and linear flight, etc.). Different route template types contain different elements.
The example file of `template.kml` is as follows (take the waypoint flight template as an example):
  <!-- Step 3: Setup A Folder for Waypoint Template -->
    <wpml:templateType>waypoint</wpml:templateType>
    <wpml:globalWaypointHeadingParam>

      <wpml:waypointHeadingAngle>45</wpml:waypointHeadingAngle>

    </wpml:globalWaypointHeadingParam>

The results do cover 2026 drone regulation changes, mainly in the UK. From January 1, 2026, all new drones placed on the UK market will use the UK class-marking system (UK0 to UK6). Previously, you needed a Flyer ID for drones 250g and above; from 2026, that drops to 100g. Remote ID: Mandatory as of 1 January 2026, for all new UK class-marked drones in classes UK1, UK2 and UK3.

These rules affect which drones you can fly, but they don't determine which drones a flight-planning app supports. For that, check the vendors' own pages: the UgCS and Map Pilot Pro websites, or their release notes and compatibility lists. The search covered KMZ/KML import into DJI Pilot 2 and Flight Hub 2, but not the oblique, terrain-follow, or DSM-file parts of your query, so I can't confirm those.

- **Pilot 2 import path:** One guide describes the route import as going through the controller's Flight Route menu, where you pick "Import Route (KMZ/KML)," choose the file from storage, and then finish planning from the route card in your mission library. Tap Import Route (KMZ/KML). A separate guide gives the menu path as Route, then KMZ Import, then storage. Import path: Route > KMZ Import > Select Table of Contents > Internal Storage/SD Card > Locate the KML/KMZ file.
- **Accepted formats:** Pilot 2 handles polygon boundaries and waypoint missions from KML/KMZ files, per one source. The DJI Pilot 2 App supports importing polygon boundaries and waypoint missions for pre-planned aerial photography with KML/KMZ files.
- **Known limits:** Pilot 2 reportedly can't import several KMZ files at once. The DJI Pilot 2 APP does not support importing multiple KMZ files at the same time. However, we can use DJI FlightHub 2 to sync KMZ files. One forum user reported a plain KML exported from Google Earth failed with a "Task Type Error," and that a converter-generated KMZ in the DJI waypoint format worked. Another said POIs are limited, noting Max one POI is supported in DJI Pilot 2.
- **Flight Hub 2:** Flight Hub 2 is the usual path for creating and exporting these files. One user reported that they created a mission in Flight Hub 2 and exported a KMZ file from that mission. They then imported the KMZ to their controller which worked fine. A third-party vendor describes UgCS allowing exporting routes as WPML files (in *.KMZ format), which can then be imported directly into DJI Pilot 2.

Not covered by these results: whether Pilot 2 or Flight Hub 2 supports oblique or smart-oblique missions, terrain-follow settings, or DSM file import. A follow-up search on those specifically would be needed.

== QGC plan doc
# Plan File Format
Plan files are stored in JSON file format and contain mission, geofence, and rally point objects.
The geofence and rally point objects are required, but their lists can be empty.
Below you can see the top level format of a Plan file
::: tip
This is "near-minimal" - a plan must contain at least one mission item.
The plan fence and rally points are also used in modes when no mission is running.
:::
```json
{
  "fileType": "Plan",
  "geoFence": {
    "circles": [],
    "polygons": [],
    "version": 2
  },
  "groundStation": "QGroundControl",
  "mission": {},
  "rallyPoints": {
    "points": [],
    "version": 2
  },
  "version": 1
}
```
The main fields are:
| Key                            | Description                                                                    |
| ------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `version`                      | The version for this file. Current version is 1.                               |
| `fileType`                     | Must be `"Plan"`.                                                              |
| `groundStation`                | The name of the ground station which created this file (here _QGroundControl_) |
| [`mission`](#mission)          | The mission associated with this flight plan.                                  |
| [`geoFence`](#geofence)        | Required geofence object. Its circles and polygons lists may be empty.         |
| [`rallyPoints`](#rally_points) | Required rally point object. Its points list may be empty.                     |
## Mission Object {#mission}
The structure of the mission object is shown below.
The `items` field contains a comma-separated list of mission items (it must contain at least one mission item, as shown below).
The list may be a mix of both [SimpleItem](#mission_simple_item) and [ComplexItem](#mission_complex_item) objects.
```json
    "mission": {
        "cruiseSpeed": 15,
        "firmwareType": 12,
        "globalPlanAltitudeMode": 1,
        "hoverSpeed": 5,
        "items": [
            {
                "AMSLAltAboveTerrain": null,
                "Altitude": 50,
                "AltitudeMode": 0,
                "autoContinue": true,
                "command": 22,
                "doJumpId": 1,
                "frame": 3,
                "params": [
                    15,
                    0,
                    0,
                    null,
                    47.3985099,
                    8.5451002,

== ODM GCP
3 1552 IMG_0585 . jpg 544033.4 5320876.0 5 1606 2763 IMG_0690 . jpg If you supply a GCP file called gcp_list.txt then ODM will automatically detect it. If it has another name you can specify using --gcp <path> . If you have a gcp file and want to do georeferencing with exif instead, then you can specify --use-exif . If you have high precision GPS measurements in your images (RTK) and want to use that information along with a gcp file, you can specify --force-gps . This post has some information about placing Ground Control Targets before a flight , but if you already have images, you can find your own points in the images post facto. It’s important that you find high-contrast objects that are found in at least 3 photos, and that you find a minimum of 5 objects. Sharp corners are good picks for GCPs. You should also place/find the GCPs evenly around your survey area. The gcp_list.txt file must be created in the base of your project folder. For good results your file should have a minimum of 15 lines after the header (5 points with 3 images to each point). User Interfaces  You can use one of two user interfaces for creating GCP files: POSM GCPi GCP Editor Pro POSM GCPi  The POSM GCPi is loaded by default on WebODM. An example is available at the WebODM Demo . To use this with known ground control XYZ values, one would do the following: Create a GCP list that only includes gcp n

WebODM did split from OpenDroneMap in April 2026. The primary announcement itself didn't appear in these results, and I found no migration guide or license details for LGT in 2026.

- **Split date and status:** The WebODM GitHub README says the project has officially decoupled from OpenDroneMap, and that it is not affiliated with OpenDroneMap and is not a user interface to OpenDroneMap. The OpenDroneMap community forum post describes Piero's announcement of WebODM leaving the OpenDroneMap banner to its own independent project. A Wikipedia reference dates the WebODM announcement to Apr 6, 2026.
- **ODX engine:** One secondary source says the split produced an independent project running ODX, a fork of the original ODM engine. I couldn't confirm this from an official source.
- **Migration details:** One third-party blog says Docker images now use the webodm/ namespace, and that the old opendronemap/ images still exist but won't receive updates from the fork. It also points to docs.webodm.org for WebODM documentation, while the legacy OpenDroneMap docs may not reflect WebODM-specific changes going forward. Treat this as unverified until checked against the official announcement at webodm.org/blog/announcement.
- **LGT:** A Gitee mirror of the README lists the engines as ODM, MicMac and LGT, with LGT linked to webodm.net. It doesn't say what LGT is or how it is licensed.
- **License:** The WebODM README states that WebODM is licensed under the terms of the GNU Affero General Public License v3.0.

The main gap is the official migration guide and any 2026 licensing change for LGT. Those would need a direct check of webodm.org and docs.webodm.org.

The owner must confirm with the authority before any flight.\n\n## ملخص عربي (10 سطور)\n\n1. المنتج اللي فاكره من Autodesk هو **ReCap Pro ومعاه ReCap Photo** (كان اسمه ReMake وقبله Memento/123D Catch). لسه موجود: فيه إصدارات ReCap Pro 2027 في 2026، بس التصوير الفوتوجرامتري فيه سحابي ومحدود، ومش أحسن اختيار للمساحة.\n2. أحسن مسار مجاني ومفتوح لـ T.O X: **WebODM + NodeODM/NodeODX** على الجهاز، والـ bridge المحلي يكلّمه بـ REST. خلّي بالك: WebODM انفصل عن OpenDroneMap في أبريل 2026 وبقى له محرك اسمه ODX (اتأكدت النهارده).\n3. T.O X يقدر يطلّع ملفات الطيران من المتصفح مباشرة: **KMZ بصيغة DJI WPML** (لـ Pilot 2 / FlightHub 2)، **Litchi CSV**، **QGroundControl .plan**، **Mission Planner .waypoints**، وKML وPDF. الطيار يحمّل الملف وبس.\n4. DJI Fly (Mini 4 Pro / Air 3) مفيهوش استيراد رسمي؛ فيه حيلة استبدال ملف KMZ غير رسمية وممكن تتعطل مع أي تحديث.\n5. الحسابات كلها بسيطة: GSD = الارتفاع × حجم البكسل ÷ البعد البؤري. مثال محلول: Mavic 3E على 75 م يدّي 2 سم/بكسل، وموقع 400×300 م = 11 خط و297 صورة وحوالي 12 دقيقة.\n6. الدقة: من غير نقط تحكم ولا RTK الخطأ المطلق بالمتر. مع RTK/PPK أو نقط تحكم (5–10 نقط + نقط تحقق مستقلة) توصل 3–5 سم. لازم تقرير RMSE من نقط التحقق مش من نقط التحكم.\n7. بيانات مجانية: Sentinel-2 (10 م)، Landsat (30 م)، Copernicus DEM GLO-30. اتأكدت النهارده إن بعض المصادر بتفتح من الصفحة مباشرة (Open-Meteo، تايلز Terrarium، STAC بتاع Element84، NASA GIBS) وبعضها محتاج الـ bridge (ملفات Copernicus DEM وWorldCover على S3 من غير CORS).\n8. القمر الصناعي مايديش عمق تحت الأرض ولا تحت المية. ده مسح منفصل: GPR للمرافق المدفونة، وسونار أو ليدار أخضر للأعماق.\n9. التصاريح: مصر = قانون 216 لسنة 2017 وتصريح من وزارة الدفاع. السعودية = الهيئة العامة للطيران المدني GACA ومنصة uas.gaca.gov.sa. لازم تتأكد بنفسك من الجهة قبل أي طيران.\n10. أول 15 حاجة تتبني مترتبة في القسم 8، وأولها: حاسبة GSD + جدول الكاميرات + شبكة الخطوط على الخريطة + تصدير KMZ/WPML.\n\n---\n\n## 0. What this session verified (short list)\n\n| Fact | Result | Label |\n|---|---|---|\n| Autodesk product | ReCap Pro with ReCap Photo. Help pages list \"ReCap Pro and ReCap Photo 2026\" and 2027 release notes with builds in April, June and October 2026. No end-of-life notice found | [V 1,2] |\n| RealityCapture | Renamed **RealityScan 2.0** (Epic, June 2025). Free under USD 1 million annual gross revenue; above that a paid seat. EULA lets Epic use scan data for training by default, opt-out in settings | [V 3,4,5] |\n| WebODM | Split from OpenDroneMap (announcement dated April 2026). New org `WebODM` on GitHub with `ODX` (engine fork), `NodeODX`, `PyODX`. WebODM 4.0.2, AGPL-3.0 | [V 6,7,8] |\n| OpenDroneMap ODM | Still active: v3.6.2 (2026-08-12), AGPL-3.0. NodeODM v3.6.2 (2026-09-24) | [V 9] |\n| NodeODM REST API | `/task/new`, `/task/new/init`, `/task/new/upload/{uuid}`, `/task/new/commit/{uuid}`, `/task/{uuid}/info`, `/task/{uuid}/output`, `/task/{uuid}/download/{asset}`, `/task/cancel`, `/task/remove`, `/task/restart`, `/task/list`, `/info`, `/options`, `/auth/*` | [V 10] |\n| DJI WPML | KMZ = ZIP holding `wpmz/template.kml`, `wpmz/waylines.wpml`, `wpmz/res/`. Copernicus DEM S3 bucket, ESA WorldCover S3 bucket, OpenTopoData public API: no CORS header | [V 15] |\n| Zenmuse P1 | 35.9 x 24 mm, 8192 x 5460, 4.4 um, 24/35/50 mm lenses, DJI claim 3 cm H / 5 cm V without GCP at 3 cm GSD | [V 16] |\n| Matrice 350 RTK | 55 min max, no payload, 8 m/s, no wind, to 0% battery | [V 17] |\n| KSA geodesy | KSA-GRF17 (EPSG:9332/9333), KSA-VRF14, KSA-GEOID17 (not a public download), KSA-CORS 209 stations | [V 18,19] |\n| ASPRS Edition 2 | Minimum check points raised from 20 to 30; survey error of check points is included in the result | [V 20] |\n\n---\n\n## 1. Photogrammetry and reality-capture software\n\n### 1.1 The Autodesk product\n\n**Autodesk ReCap Pro** (desktop, Windows) with its companion **ReCap Photo**. History [M]: 123D Catch and Project Memento became ReMake (2016), and ReMake became ReCap Photo in 2017 [V 21]. ReCap Pro is mainly for registering **laser scans** (RCP/RCS point clouds for Revit, Civil 3D, Navisworks). ReCap Photo handles **photos** and processes them in Autodesk's cloud [M].\n\nStatus today: ReCap Pro 2026 ships with ReCap Photo, and Autodesk help has 2027 release notes [V 1,2]. I found no retirement notice. Limits I remember but did not re-read today: cloud processing with photo-count caps and cloud credits, no local processing, weak GCP/check-point reporting compared with survey tools [M]. Price not found. Conclusion: good as the bridge into Revit/Civil 3D point-cloud formats, not as the main photogrammetry engine.\n\n### 1.2 Commercial software\n\n| Software | Licence / status | Platform | Inputs | Outputs | GCP / RTK / PPK | API or CLI a helper could drive | Accuracy claim and source | Hardware |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Autodesk ReCap Pro + ReCap Photo | Subscription, proprietary [V 1]. Price not found | Windows | Photos (ReCap Photo), laser scans (ReCap Pro) | RCP/RCS, RCM mesh, OBJ, ortho [M] | GCP supported in ReCap Photo aerial projects [M] | No documented local CLI [M] | None read today | Cloud does the work [M] |\n| Bentley iTwin Capture Modeler (was ContextCapture) | Proprietary. \"Flex\" edition free for setup and editing; processing needs iTwin Capture Engine or cloud subscription [V 22]. Price not found | Windows + cloud | Photos, video, lidar | 3MX, 3SM, OBJ, FBX, I3S, OSGB, Cesium 3D Tiles (reseller list) [V 23], LAS, ortho/DSM [M] | GCP, check points, RTK/PPK [M] | iTwin Capture SDK (Python) and cloud APIs [V 22] | None read today | Strong GPU, scales to clusters [M] |\n| Agisoft Metashape Professional 2.x | Perpetual licence, node-locked or floating. Version 2.3.0 current [V 24]. Third-party prices seen today: USD 3,499 (reseller), EUR 3,999 incl. VAT (reseller) [V 25] | Windows, macOS, Linux | Photos, video frames, lidar, multispectral | LAS/LAZ, OBJ/PLY/FBX, Cesium 3D Tiles, GeoTIFF ortho, DSM/DTM, contours [M] | GCP + check points with error table, RTK/PPK camera accuracy [M] | **Python API**, headless `metashape -r script.py`, Java API [V 25 reseller text; M for the command] | None read today | NVIDIA or AMD GPU, 32 GB+ RAM for 500+ photos [M] |\n| Pix4D (PIX4Dmatic, PIX4Dmapper, PIX4Dcloud) | Subscription. PIX4Dmatic now has Standard, Analyst and Pro plans and absorbed PIX4Dsurvey [V 26]. A third-party page says PIX4Dmapper sales end 2027-01-01 and support ends 2027-12-01 [V 27, not confirmed on pix4d.com]. Prices rose from 2026-01-05 [V 28].
